# About this item

*   Compatible with Nintendo-Switch (NOT Nintendo-Switch 2)
*   Save time with card offload speeds of up to 190MB/s powered by SanDisk QuickFlow Technology (Up to 190MB/s read speeds, engineered with proprietary technology to reach speeds beyond UHS-I 104MB/s, requires compatible devices capable of reaching such speeds. Based on internal testing; performance may be lower depending upon host device interface, usage conditions and other factors. 1MB=1,000,000 bytes. SanDisk QuickFlow Technology is only available for 64GB, 128GB, 256GB, 400GB, 512GB, and 1TB capacities. 1GB=1,000,000,000 bytes and 1TB=1,000,000,000,000 bytes. Actual user storage less.)
*   Pair with the SanDisk Professional PRO-READER SD and microSD to achieve maximum speeds (sold separately)
*   Up to 130MB/s write speeds for fast shooting (Based on internal testing; performance may be lower depending upon host device interface, usage conditions and other factors. 1MB=1,000,000 bytes.)
*   4K and 5K UHD-ready with UHS Speed Class 3 (U3) and Video Speed Class 30 (V30) (Compatible device required. Full HD (1920x1080), 4K UHD (3840 x 2160), and 5K UHD (5120 X 2880) support may vary based upon host device, file attributes and other factors. See HD page on SanDisk site. UHS Speed Class 3 (U3) designates a performance option designed to support real-time video recording with UHS-enabled host devices. Video Speed Class 30 (V30), sustained video capture rate of 30MB/s, designates a performance option designed to support real-time video recording with UHS-enabled host devices. See the SD Association’s official website.)
*   Rated A2 for faster loading and in-app performance (A2 performance is 4000 read IOPS, 2000 write IOPS. Results may vary based on host device, app type and other factors)

## From the manufacturer

     

![M01](https://images-na.ssl-images-amazon.com/images/G/01/x-locale/common/grey-pixel.gif)

![M02-1TB](https://images-na.ssl-images-amazon.com/images/G/01/x-locale/common/grey-pixel.gif)

# Write Speeds of up to 130MB/s(1)

Capture fast-action photos or shoot 4K and 5K UHD video(3) with write speeds of up to 130MB/s. The SANDISK Extreme microSDXC UHS-I Card lets you shoot more, fast. \[256GB-1TB\]

[](javascript:void\(0\))

The video showcases the product in use.The video guides you through product setup.The video compares multiple products.The video shows the product being unpacked.

# SANDISK Extreme microSDXC card 128GB Video

 

![M04-1TB](https://images-na.ssl-images-amazon.com/images/G/01/x-locale/common/grey-pixel.gif)

# Improve Your Workflow Efficiency

With SANDISK QuickFlow Technology(2), this card optimizes performance with quick read speeds up to 190MB/s. Pair with the SANDISK Professional PRO-READER SD and microSD to achieve maximum speeds (sold separately).\[128GB-1TB\]

![M05-Feature 1](https://images-na.ssl-images-amazon.com/images/G/01/x-locale/common/grey-pixel.gif)

# Shoots 4K and 5K Video(3)

This card captures 4K and 5K UHD and Full HD video with its UHS Speed Class 3 (U3) and Video Speed Class 30 (V30) ratings.(4)

![M05-Feature 2](https://images-na.ssl-images-amazon.com/images/G/01/x-locale/common/grey-pixel.gif)

# Load Apps with A2(5)

Get faster app performance for an outstanding smartphone experience thanks to this card’s A2 Specification.\[64GB-1TB\]

![M05-Feature 3](https://images-na.ssl-images-amazon.com/images/G/01/x-locale/common/grey-pixel.gif)

# Durable Design

SANDISK Extreme microSDHC and microSDXC UHS-I Cards are shockproof, temperature-proof, waterproof, and X-ray-proof(6).

![M05-Feature-4-1TB](https://images-na.ssl-images-amazon.com/images/G/01/x-locale/common/grey-pixel.gif)

# Easy File Recovery

Includes a 2-year offer for RescuePRO Deluxe file recovery software(7), which helps you restore images you accidentally deleted.

# Legal disclaimers

Not all devices support microSD memory card formats. Check with your device manufacturer for more details.

(1) Speeds vary by capacity. \[256GB-1TB\]: Up to 190MB/s read speeds, engineered with proprietary technology to reach speeds beyond UHS-I 104MB/s, requires compatible devices capable of reaching such speeds. Up to 130MB/s write speeds. Based on internal testing; performance may be lower depending upon host device interface, usage conditions and other factors. 1MB=1,000,000 bytes. \[128GB\]: Up to 190MB/s read speeds, engineered with proprietary technology to reach speeds beyond UHS-I 104MB/s, requires compatible devices capable of reaching such speeds. Up to 90MB/s write speeds. Based on internal testing; performance may be lower depending upon host device interface, usage conditions and other factors. 1MB=1,000,000 bytes. \[64GB\]: Up to 170MB/s read speeds, engineered with proprietary technology to reach speeds beyond UHS-I 104MB/s, requires compatible devices capable of reaching such speeds. Up to 80MB/s write speeds. Based on internal testing; performance may be lower depending upon host device interface, usage conditions and other factors. 1MB=1,000,000 bytes. \[32GB\]: Up to 100MB/s read speeds; up to 60MB/s write speeds. Based on internal testing; performance may be lower depending upon host device interface, usage conditions and other factors. 1MB=1,000,000 bytes. (2) SANDISK QuickFlow Technology is only available for 64GB, 128GB, 256GB, 400GB, 512GB, and 1TB capacities. Must use SANDISK Professional PRO-READER SD and microSD to achieve maximum transfer speeds (sold separately). (3) Compatible device required. Full HD (1920x1080), 4K UHD (3840 x 2160), and 5K UHD (5120 X 2880) support may vary based upon host device, file attributes and other factors. See HD page on Sandisk site. (4) UHS Speed Class 3 (U3) designates a performance option designed to support real-time video recording with UHS-enabled host devices. Video Speed Class 30 (V30), sustained video capture rate of 30MB/s, designates a performance option designed to support real-time video recording with UHS-enabled host devices. See the SD Association’s official website. (5) \[64GB-1TB\]: A2 performance is 4000 read IOPS, 2000 write IOPS. Results may vary based on host device, app type and other factors. \[32GB\]: A1 performance is 1500 read IOPS, 500 write IOPS. Results may vary based on host device, app type and other factors. (6) Card only. See official Sandisk website for additional information and limitations. (7) Registration required; terms and conditions apply. (8) Download and installation required. See the SANDISK Memory Zone page on Sandisk site. (9) 1GB=1,000,000,000 bytes and 1TB=1,000,000,000,000 bytes. Actual user storage less.

Product specifications subject to change without notice. Pictures shown may vary from actual products.

SANDISK, the SANDISK logo, SANDISK Professional, SANDISK Extreme, SANDISK QuickFlow, and RescuePRO are registered marks or marks of Sandisk Corporation or its affiliates in the US and/or other countries. The microSD, microSDHC and microSDXC marks and logos are marks of SD-3C, LLC. Android is a mark of Google LLC. All other marks are the property of their respective owners.

## Product Description

The SanDisk Extreme microSDXC memory card lets you save time transferring media with read speeds of up to 190MB/s(1) powered by SanDisk QuickFlow Technology(2). Pair with the SanDisk Professional PRO-READER SD and microSD to achieve maximum speeds (sold separately). With write speeds up to 130MB/s(1) it’s ideal for your Android smartphone, action cameras, or drones. This high-performance microSD card handles 4K and 5K UHD video recording, Full HD video(3), and high-resolution photos. | (1)Up to 190MB/s read speeds, engineered with proprietary technology to reach speeds beyond UHS-I 104MB/s, requires compatible devices capable of reaching such speeds. Up to 130MB/s write speeds. Based on internal testing; performance may be lower depending upon host device interface, usage conditions and other factors. 1MB=1,000,000 bytes. (2)SanDisk QuickFlow Technology is only available for 64GB, 128GB, 256GB, 400GB, 512GB, and 1TB capacities. \[1GB=1,000,000,000 bytes and 1TB=1,000,000,000,000 bytes.\] Actual user storage less. (3)Compatible device required. Full HD (1920x1080) and 4K UHD (3840 x 2160), and 5K UHD (5120 X 2880) support may vary based upon host device, file attributes and other factors. See HD page on SanDisk site.

[How customer reviews and ratings work](javascript:void\(0\))

Customer Reviews, including Product Star Ratings help customers to learn more about the product and decide whether it is the right product for them.

To calculate the overall star rating and percentage breakdown by star, we don’t use a simple average. Instead, our system considers things like how recent a review is and if the reviewer bought the item on Amazon. It also analyzed reviews to verify trustworthiness.

[Learn more how customers reviews work on Amazon](/gp/help/customer/display.html/ref=cm_cr_dp_d_omni_lm_btn?nodeId=G8UYX7LALQC8V9KA)

### Customers say

Customers find the microSD card to be a stellar choice with fast read and write speeds, ample storage capacity for photos, and compatibility with various devices including DJI mavic pro, Nintendo Switch, and Steam Deck. They appreciate its value for money and versatility for action cameras, drones, and mobile devices. While some customers report no issues with reliability and durability, others mention the card failing within two weeks of use.

Amazon Customer

](javascript:void\(0\))

_5 out of 5 stars_

##### MicroSD 2TB comparison - Lexar vs SanDisk

I tested the 2TB version of a SanDisk Extreme card against the Lexar Play Blue in a 2024 MacBook Pro 14 M4 Pro system, using an old PiDrive adapter in the onboard SD Card Reader. If you've researched system specs, you'll know the 2024 MBP has a uhs-ii controller that should theoretically support fairly fast SD speeds (250MB/s). But there are very few uhs-ii cards around, and no cards with a 1TB or 2TB capacity at the time I was shopping. So I was looking for 2TB which forced me to settle for uhs-i and these two options. I read some slower transfer speed disappointment reviews associated with a PNY Pro Elite Prime card so I eliminated that one (which comes in a 1.5TB version) and then everything else was eliminated based on capacity and/or reputation. For example, I really wanted to try the TeamGroup T-create card, but there were a few reviews that talked about excess heat and throttling under sustained load. I also really wanted to try a Lexar Silver 1066x because the Lexar products sometimes have a slight performance edge in some reviews, but the silver tops out at 1TB (I think) so that is what brought the Lexar Play Blue, as it looked like the closest thing from Lexar that might have a similarly performing underlying NAND if it were to somehow be using the same component as the Silver 1066. I was really tempted by the Lexar Gold, as well as Nextorage and a Sabrent devices that all have uhs-ii specifications, but none of them are available at capacities above 512GB, which just wasn't enough for my interest. So I ran black magic against the SanDisk and Lexar, with the SanDisk Extreme performing at 86MB write, 90MB read speeds (I'll reiterate without a SanDisk adapter). The Lexar Play Blue generated 55MB write, 90MB read. While this isn't exhaustive, the SanDisk performance matches the best numbers I've been able to identify elsewhere, and typically those numbers are reported on SanDisk. Consequently, the SanDisk performance numbers seem like they may be the maximum that is available for NAND flash storage substrates in microsd form factors of 1TB and above (barring using SanDisk's adapter/controller). To this end, there is Reddit thread here and there with folks testing different MicroSD Express devices in Nintendo Switch 2 units - which is a newer interface specification - and even those tests seem to top out around 94MB/s to 96MB/s. Granted, in write performance that's 10% improvement over what the MBP generated, but I'm not chasing 10%. I'd chase a different product if something were capable of 25% greater performance (110MB/s range), but I don't think anything currently coming out of the factories with 1TB+ capacity is going to demonstrate that difference. I'll note BaseQI makes a uhs-ii MBP SD slot adapter like the PiDrive I have (I think it was possibly originally a BaseQI product back then), which should theoretically be capable of higher performance, but I think this is going to require both different NAND as well as the uhs-ii extra pin control paths, and neither of these are available in 1TB+ microSD form factors today (from what I can tell). If you're looking for a card for a MBP, then the SanDisk Extreme is it for you.

The read and write speeds are fast, making it easy to record high-quality video and capture photos without worrying about dropped frames or slow transfers.

    Reviewed in the United States on May 21, 2026

    Brief content visible, double tap to read full content.
    
    Full content visible, double tap to read brief content.
    
    Sandisk is a gold standard of memory cards for all sort of devices and a brand that I have trusted over the decades to handle my pictures and video. The 128GB is sufficient for my Osmo Pocket 3 and Action 5 Pro cameras and works well with GoPro's and other cameras upto 4K that use this card slot.
    
    These work best when plugged in directly but the included SD Sled is also possible to use in cameras with the large slot size.
    
    This is not the fastest card they make, but it probably is the middle upper one you should have. The Extreme Pro's tend to cost a bit more and are a bit faster in the write speed, which can help in some cameras when shooting very dynamic motion shots like sports, etc.
    
    As an everyday use card, these hold up much better than the SD versions and usually live in the camera on a permanent basis, as I access the files through apps or software removing the need to handle the card too much.
    
    If you need a middle ground well made and reliable micro SD card, let it be Sandisk, let is be this one.

    I bought this in Nov. of 2024 for use in a cell phone, and it worked great. I later upgraded to a new phone that does not have a micro SD slot, so this was stored in an anti-static bag until I got a Bambu Lab A1 3D printer. I inserted this into the printer which came with a much smaller 16GB card, and the printer recognized it immediately. When I bought it, the price was less than half the price of what it currently is in Aug., 2026. If you need this yesterday, it's a great card, but if not, I strongly recommend waiting for the prices to go down.

    I purchased this back in December for just $160, quite astonished by the price jump now, no doubt virtue of AI. I use mine in my HiFi Walker H2 with Rockbox; it works perfectly out-of-the-box and thanks to the microSD port I have on my Framework laptop, I have no need of an adapter to transfer files between my laptop and Rockbox. The capacity can't be beat and though I have not personally tested its speed, database initializing and updating on Rockbox tends to be pretty snappy. My only regret now is not buying a second at the time for my camera, too! I'd \*probably\* still buy the 2 TB in today's economy if needs must but it isn't the value for the money it once was.

    Je suis ravie d'avoir pu remettre une micro sd dans mon téléphone. SD est une valeur sûre pour moi, je n'ai jamais eu de soucis avec la marque. J'avais acheté une sous marque en magasin de la même capacité et elle n'a pas tenu 1 an. Je n'ai plus accès à certains éléments qui étaient sur la carte de sous marque. Je vais rester sur un rapport qualité prix qui est sûr.
    
    J'ai pu sauvegarder tous ce que j'avais à sauvegarder. J'envisage d'ailleurs d'en reprendre une autre d'une capacité plus importante.
